A numbers-only reconstruction of Anthropic, PBC’s Form S-1/A · Amendment No. 1 — filed with the SEC on November 2, 2026 (accession 0001628280-26-068676), built for printed side-by-side comparison with the filed document.

Included: every dollar figure, percentage, ratio, share count, commitment, table row, per-share amount, and stock-plan term present in the filing — in the filing’s original section order, with verbatim source quotes and line anchors into the extracted filing text (sx1a_extracted.txt).

Omitted: all narrative, risk discussion, mission prose, and boilerplate. Withheld values appear as highlighted [BLANK] markers.

Page numbering follows the filing’s convention: front matter in roman numerals; body in arabic numerals; financial statements and notes as F-pages; Part II as II-pages.

Use of proceedsWe estimate that the net proceeds from the sale of shares of our Class A common stock by us in this offering will be approximately $ (or approximately $ if the underwriters option to purchase additional shares of our Class A common stock is exercised in full), based upon the assumed initial public offering price of $ per share, which is the midpoint of the estimated offering price range set forth on the cover page of this prospectus, and after deducting estimated underwriting discounts and commissions and estimated offering expenses payable by us. We will not receive any proceeds from the sale of shares of Class A common stock by the selling stockholders. The principal purposes of this offering are to increase our capitalization and financial flexibility, create a public market for our Class A common stock, facilitate an orderly distribution of shares for the selling stockholders, and enable access to the public equity markets for us and our stockholders. We intend to use the net proceeds from this offering for general corporate purposes, including working capital, operating expenses, and capital expenditures, including investments in compute and infrastructure, as well as hiring and retention of highly skilled personnel. Additionally, we may use a portion of the net proceeds to acquire or invest in businesses, products, services, or technologies. However, we do not have agreements or commitments for any material acquisitions or investments at this time. We also intend to use a portion of the net proceeds to satisfy our anticipated tax withholding and remittance obligations related to the settlement of certain of our outstanding RSUs. See the section titled Use of Proceeds for more information.
Voting rightsShares of our Class A common stock are entitled to one vote per share. Shares of our Class N common stock have no voting rights, except as otherwise required by law. The share of our Class T common stock has no voting rights with respect to matters submitted to stockholders generally, except as set forth within our amended and restated certificate of incorporation, including with respect to the election of a majority of our board of directors or as required by law. The share of our Class F common stock will be entitled to an aggregate number of votes that equals 50.1% of the total voting power of our outstanding shares of capital stock as of the applicable record date on all matters submitted to a vote of stockholders generally, including the election of our directors other than the Class T directors. Shares of our Class E common stock will be entitled to one vote per share with respect to certain limited matters.
Controlled companyWe are considered a controlled company within the meaning of the Nasdaq Global Select Market corporate governance requirements. See the sections titled Our Governance Structure and Its Goals, Management, and Description of Capital Stock for additional information.
Risk factorsSee the section titled Risk Factors and other information included in this prospectus for a discussion of some of the factors you should consider before deciding to invest in our Class A common stock.

Sunset Provisions
ItemClass T Sunset TimeClass F Sunset Time
TriggerThe Class T Sunset Time occurs when no Class T common stock remains outstanding, including due to the redemption of our Class T common stock or an amendment to the rights of our Class T common stock that eliminates our Class T common stock, which in each case requires the approval of persons comprising the Trust Amendment Threshold. The Trust Amendment Threshold may be satisfied as follows if the Trustees are voting in favor of the action, upon approval of 80% of the authorized members of our board of directors and 80% of the Trustees then in office (in each case, rounded up or down to the nearest whole number (any exact 0.5 rounding up)) if our Class F common stock remains outstanding and the Trustees are not voting in favor of the action until the third anniversary of the completion of this offering, upon approval by (i) our Class F common stock acting pursuant to the unanimous approval of the Continuing Founders, (ii) a majority of the votes cast by our Class E common stock (subject to a quorum of 40% of our outstanding Class E common stock), and (iii) a majority of the votes cast by outstanding Class A common stock (subject to a quorum of a majority of outstanding Class A common stock) or at any time, upon approval by (i) the Class F common stock acting by decision of 66 23% of the Continuing Founders (rounded up to the nearest whole number), (ii) 75% of the votes cast by our Class E common stock (subject to a quorum of a majority of outstanding Class E common stock), and (iii) a majority of the votes cast by our Class A common stock (subject to a quorum of a majority of outstanding Class A common stock) or if no Class F common stock remains outstanding and the Trustees are not voting in favor of the action, upon approval of 85% of our outstanding Class A common stock. The Class T Sunset Time does not occur automatically upon the lapse of time or the occurrence of specific events. Any action resulting in the occurrence of the Class T Sunset Time requires approval of persons comprising the Trust Amendment Threshold. The Class T Sunset Time, once triggered, is not subject to extension, delay, or waiver.The Class F Sunset Time occurs on the earlier of (i) the time when no Class F common stock remains outstanding, including due to the redemption of our Class F common stock agreed to by a Supermajority of the Continuing Founders or upon the dissolution of the Founder LLC, and (ii) the completion of the Class F Transition Period. The Class F Transition Period commences when there have been fewer than three Continuing Founders (not including any successor member who is a Non-Qualifying Member as defined in the Founder LLC Agreement) for 90 consecutive calendar days.
ContinuationA Continuing Founder will be removed from the Founder LLC upon the earliest to occur of (i) such Continuing Founders resignation as a member of the Founder LLC (ii) such Continuing Founders death or incapacity (iii) with respect to each of our seven original co-founders, such original co-founders ceasing to hold (together with such original co-founders related holders) on any record date for a vote of our stockholders a number of shares of Class A common stock (including shares subject to options, RSUs, and other convertible securities) equal to at least 2% of the shares of Class A common stock held by such original co-founder and their related holders as of the completion of this offering (iv) such Continuing Founders removal for cause, which includes specified competitive activities described in the Founder LLC Agreement (v) with respect to any successor member admitted to the Founder LLC prior to the commencement of the Class F Transition Period, other than a successor member who is a member during the Class F Transition Period, the tenth anniversary of the completion of this offering or (vi) with respect to each of our seven original co-founders, the commencement of a leave of absence approved by a Supermajority of the other Continuing Founders, which may not be approved during the Class F Transition Period or if the grant of the leave of absence would itself cause the Class F Transition Period to commence. Removals due to resignation, death, or the occurrence of the tenth anniversary of the completion of this offering occur automatically without any determination by any other person. Removals due to incapacity require determination by a qualified physician jointly selected by the affected Continuing Founder and a majority of the other Continuing Founders (in the case of physical or mental impairment) or determination by a Supermajority of the other Continuing Founders (in the case of wrongful imprisonment, unlawful detention, or captivity or restraint), subject to the affected Continuing Founders right to contest such determination. Removals due to cause require an affirmative determination by a Supermajority of the other Continuing Founders, subject to the affected Continuing Founders right to contest such determination or, in the case of specified categories of cause, to cure the basis for such removal. Removals due to incapacity, cause, or the failure to maintain the minimum ownership threshold require prior written notice from the Founder LLC by action of a majority of the other Continuing Founders. If a Continuing Founder is removed due to death or incapacity during the first ten years following the completion of this offering, a successor member may be admitted to the Founder LLC and will count toward the three Continuing Founders required to avoid triggering the Class F Transition Period until the tenth anniversary of the completion of this offering. The Class F Sunset Time, once triggered, is not subject to extension, delay, or waiver.
Changes in Voting Power and Director Election and Removal RightsPrior to the Class T Sunset Time, the LTBT, as the sole holder of Class T common stock, will generally have the authority to elect and remove the Class T directors, who constitute a majority of the authorized members of our board of directors. The share of our Class T common stock has no voting rights with respect to matters submitted to stockholders generally, except as set forth within our amended and restated certificate of incorporation, including with respect to the election of a majority of our board of directors or as required by law. Following the Class T Sunset Time, if our Class F common stock remains outstanding, our Class A common stock and Class F common stock, voting together as a single class, will elect all of our directors. Because our Class F common stock is entitled to an aggregate number of votes equal to 50.1% of the total voting power of our outstanding shares of capital stock, the Founder LLC would have the ability to determine the outcome of all matters submitted to a vote of stockholders generally, including the election of all directors. If neither our Class T common stock nor Class F common stock remains outstanding, our Class A common stock would have the ability to determine the outcome of all matters submitted to a vote of stockholders generally, including the election of all directors.Prior to the Class F Sunset Time, our Class F common stock will be entitled to an aggregate number of votes that equals 50.1% of the total voting power of our outstanding shares of capital stock as of the applicable record date and, together with our Class A common stock, voting together as a single class, will elect the directors other than the Class T directors and decide all other matters put to a vote of stockholders generally. Immediately after the Class F Sunset Time, if our Class T common stock remains outstanding, the LTBT would continue to have the authority to elect and remove the Class T directors, who would constitute a majority of the authorized members of our board of directors. However, the LTBT would no longer need to comply with the procedures for nomination, removal, or change in the Class T director designated as the Specified Class T Director. In such a case, our Class A common stock would elect and remove the remaining directors and decide all other matters put to a vote of stockholders generally. If neither our Class T common stock nor Class F common stock remains outstanding, our Class A common stock would have the ability to determine the outcome of all matters submitted to a vote of stockholders generally, including the election of all directors.

source line 26524 · transcribed by sx1a_b09
Compensation Governance Highlights
—
ItemWhat We Do Not Do
Maintain a competitive, consistent, and simple compensation philosophy to attract, retain, and motivate top executive talent.Enter into employment agreements that provide for fixed terms, automatic compensation increases, or guaranteed equity grants.
Focus our executive compensation program on at-risk compensation, which aligns stockholder and executive interests.Provide single-trigger change of control benefits or excessive cash severance.
Deliver a majority of our NEOs compensation opportunities in the form of long-term equity awards.Provide our NEOs with golden parachute excise tax reimbursement or gross-ups.
Conduct regular compensation reviews to ensure alignment with competitive market practices.Provide perquisites as a meaningful component of our executive compensation.
Provide for double-trigger change of control severance provisions for only our Chief Financial Officer, and none of our other NEOs.Maintain any executive pension plans, or any retirement programs that are not generally available to all employees.

*Represents beneficial ownership of less than 1%.

Represents the percentage of the total votes attributable to all outstanding shares of our Class A common stock and Class F common stock. Does not include (i) our Class T common stock, which is held by the LTBT, and which generally has the authority to elect and remove the Class T directors, who together will constitute a majority of the authorized members of our board of directors, and is generally not entitled to vote with respect to matters submitted to a vote of stockholders, (ii) our Class N common stock, which is not entitled to vote on any matter that is submitted to a vote of stockholders, except as otherwise required by law, or (iii) our Class E common stock, which is not entitled to vote for the election of directors.

Holders of our Class A common stock are entitled to one vote per share on all matters submitted to stockholders generally. Our Class F common stock is entitled to a fixed 50.1% of the voting power of our outstanding capital stock as of the applicable record date on matters submitted to stockholders generally. As a result, the voting power of the holders of Class A common stock is limited to an aggregate of 49.9% of the total outstanding voting power as of the applicable record date. Accordingly, the voting power of each holder of Class A common stock has been reduced to 49.9% of the percentage shown before the offering (taking into account the reduction of voting power due to shares sold in the offering).

We expect that our seven co-founders, including Dr. Amodei and Ms. Amodei, will enter into the Founder LLC Agreement shortly before the effectiveness of the registration statement of which this prospectus forms a part. The Founder LLC Agreement will provide that the share of Class F common stock will be voted, consented, or not consented, in the manner determined by a vote of the Continuing Founders as prescribed by the Founder LLC Agreement, with each Continuing Founder entitled to one vote. With respect to each of our co-founders, the voting power percentage provided does not include the voting power of our Class F common stock held by the Founder LLC. The Founder LLC Agreement will not govern the voting of any shares of our Class A common stock held by the co-founders or their related holders. Each co-founder (or their related holders) will be able to vote shares of our Class A common stock held by them in their discretion, regardless of the manner in which the Founder LLC will vote our Class F common stock. Sunset Provisions
Two content columns (Class T Sunset Time, Class F Sunset Time) with row labels in an unlabeled first column; header repeats after each printed page break (lines 30806-30808, 30916-30918) and those repetitions are skipped.
ItemClass T Sunset TimeClass F Sunset Time
TriggerThe Class T Sunset Time occurs when no Class T common stock remains outstanding, including due to the redemption of our Class T common stock or an amendment to the rights of our Class T common stock that eliminates our Class T common stock, which in each case requires the approval of persons comprising the Trust Amendment Threshold. The Trust Amendment Threshold may be satisfied as follows if the Trustees are voting in favor of the action, upon approval of 80% of the authorized members of our board of directors and 80% of the Trustees then in office (in each case, rounded up or down to the nearest whole number (any exact 0.5 rounding up)) if our Class F common stock remains outstanding and the Trustees are not voting in favor of the action until the third anniversary of the completion of this offering, upon approval by (i) our Class F common stock acting pursuant to the unanimous approval of the Continuing Founders, (ii) a majority of the votes cast by our Class E common stock (subject to a quorum of 40% of our outstanding Class E common stock), and (iii) a majority of the votes cast by outstanding Class A common stock (subject to a quorum of a majority of outstanding Class A common stock) or at any time, upon approval by (i) our Class F common stock acting by decision of 66 23% of the Continuing Founders (rounded up to the nearest whole number), (ii) 75% of the votes cast by our Class E common stock (subject to a quorum of a majority of outstanding Class E common stock), and (iii) a majority of the votes cast by our Class A common stock (subject to a quorum of a majority of outstanding Class A common stock) or if no Class F common stock remains outstanding and the Trustees are not voting in favor of the action, upon approval of 85% of our outstanding Class A common stock. The Class T Sunset Time does not occur automatically upon the lapse of time or the occurrence of specific events. Any action resulting in the occurrence of the Class T Sunset Time requires approval of persons comprising the Trust Amendment Threshold. The Class T Sunset Time, once triggered, is not subject to extension, delay, or waiver.The Class F Sunset Time occurs on the earlier of (i) the time when no Class F common stock remains outstanding, including due to the redemption of our Class F common stock agreed to by a Supermajority of the Continuing Founders or upon the dissolution of the Founder LLC, and (ii) the completion of the Class F Transition Period. The Class F Transition Period commences when there have been fewer than three Continuing Founders (not including any successor member who is a Non-Qualifying Member as defined in the Founder LLC Agreement) for 90 consecutive calendar days.
ContinuationA Continuing Founder will be removed from the Founder LLC upon the earliest to occur of (i) such Continuing Founders resignation as a member of the Founder LLC (ii) such Continuing Founders death or incapacity (iii) with respect to each of our seven original co-founders, such original co-founders ceasing to hold (together with such original co-founders related holders) on any record date for a vote of our stockholders a number of shares of Class A common stock (including shares subject to options, RSUs, and other convertible securities) equal to at least 2% of the shares of Class A common stock held by such original co-founder and their related holders as of the completion of this offering (iv) such Continuing Founders removal for cause, which includes specified competitive activities described in the Founder LLC Agreement (v) with respect to any successor member admitted to the Founder LLC prior to the commencement of the Class F Transition Period, other than a successor member who is a member during the Class F Transition Period, the tenth anniversary of the completion of this offering or (vi) with respect to each of our seven original co-founders, the commencement of a leave of absence approved by a Supermajority of the other Continuing Founders, which may not be approved during the Class F Transition Period or if the grant of the leave of absence would itself cause the Class F Transition Period to commence. Removals due to resignation, death, or the occurrence of the tenth anniversary of the completion of this offering occur automatically without any determination by any other person. Removals due to incapacity require determination by a qualified physician jointly selected by the affected Continuing Founder and a majority of the other Continuing Founders (in the case of physical or mental impairment) or determination by a Supermajority of the other Continuing Founders (in the case of wrongful imprisonment, unlawful detention, or captivity or restraint), subject to the affected Continuing Founders right to contest such determination. Removals due to cause require an affirmative determination by a Supermajority of the other Continuing Founders, subject to the affected Continuing Founders right to contest such determination or, in the case of specified categories of cause, to cure the basis for such removal. Removals due to incapacity, cause, or the failure to maintain the minimum ownership threshold require prior written notice from the Founder LLC by action of a majority of the other Continuing Founders. If a Continuing Founder is removed due to death or incapacity during the first ten years following the completion of this offering, a successor member may be admitted to the Founder LLC and will count toward the three Continuing Founders required to avoid triggering the Class F Transition Period until the tenth anniversary of the completion of this offering. The Class F Sunset Time, once triggered, is not subject to extension, delay, or waiver.
Changes in Voting Power and Director Election and Removal RightsPrior to the Class T Sunset Time, the LTBT, as the sole holder of Class T common stock, will generally have the authority to elect and remove the Class T directors, who constitute a majority of the authorized members of our board of directors. The share of our Class T common stock has no voting rights with respect to matters submitted to stockholders generally, except as set forth within our amended and restated certificate of incorporation, including with respect to the election of a majority of our board of directors or as required by law. Following the Class T Sunset Time, if our Class F common stock remains outstanding, our Class A common stock and Class F common stock, voting together as a single class, will elect all of our directors. Because our Class F common stock is entitled to an aggregate number of votes equal to 50.1% of the total voting power of our outstanding shares of capital stock, the Founder LLC would have the ability to determine the outcome of all matters submitted to a vote of stockholders generally, including the election of all directors. If neither our Class T common stock nor Class F common stock remains outstanding, our Class A common stock would have the ability to determine the outcome of all matters submitted to a vote of stockholders generally, including the election of all directors.Prior to the Class F Sunset Time, our Class F common stock will be entitled to an aggregate number of votes that equals 50.1% of the total voting power of our outstanding shares of capital stock as of the applicable record date and, together with our Class A common stock, voting together as a single class, will elect the directors other than the Class T directors and decide all other matters put to a vote of stockholders generally. Immediately after the Class F Sunset Time, if our Class T common stock remains outstanding, the LTBT would continue to have the authority to elect and remove the Class T directors, who would constitute a majority of the authorized members of our board of directors. However, the LTBT would no longer need to comply with the procedures for nomination, removal, or change in the Class T director designated as the Specified Class T Director. In such a case, our Class A common stock would elect and remove the remaining directors and decide all other matters put to a vote of stockholders generally. If neither our Class T common stock nor Class F common stock remains outstanding, our Class A common stock would have the ability to determine the outcome of all matters submitted to a vote of stockholders generally, including the election of all directors.

Class F Common Stock Voting Mechanics
—
ItemThreshold(1)If threshold is metOtherwise
General stockholder mattersMajority(2)Class F common stock is voted accordingly.Class F common stock is voted proportionately with the votes of Class A common stock cast (subject to a quorum of a majority of outstanding Class A common stock).
Amendments to Class F rightsSupermajority(3)(4)Class F common stock is voted for the proposal.Class F common stock is voted against (or withhold as to) the proposal.
Amendments to LTBT Class T rightsTrust Amendment Supermajority(4)(5)Class F common stock is voted for the proposal.Class F common stock is voted against (or withhold as to) the proposal.
Written consentThe applicable majority, Supermajority(3), or Trust Amendment Supermajority(5) if action were taken at a meeting.Consent is delivered with respect to our Class F common stock.No consent is delivered with respect to our Class F common stock.

(1)Each Continuing Founder will be entitled to one vote when determining how our Class F common stock will be voted.

(2)If a majority of the Continuing Founders deliver the same instruction as to how the votes corresponding to our Class F common stock shall be cast, including for, against, or withhold, but excluding an instruction to abstain, our Class F common stock will be voted accordingly. If a majority of the Continuing Founders deliver instructions to abstain or do not deliver the same or any instructions, our Class F common stock will be voted proportionately with the votes of Class A common stock cast (subject to a quorum of a majority of outstanding Class A common stock).

(3)Supermajority means the total number of Continuing Founders entitled to vote, minus one, delivers instructions to vote for the proposal. If there are fewer than three Continuing Founders, a Supermajority requires unanimity of Continuing Founders.

(4)If a proposal requires both a Supermajority and a Trust Amendment Supermajority, both thresholds must be satisfied.

(5)A Trust Amendment Supermajority will be satisfied if (i) 66 23% of the Continuing Founders, rounded up to the nearest whole number, deliver instructions to vote for the proposal, and approval by 75% of the votes cast by our Class E common stock (subject to a quorum of a majority of outstanding Class E common stock) and a majority of the votes cast by our Class A common stock (subject to a quorum of a majority of the outstanding shares of Class A common stock) is also obtained or (ii) until the third anniversary of the completion of this offering, the Continuing Founders unanimously deliver instructions to vote for the proposal, and approval by a majority of the votes cast by our Class E common stock (subject to a quorum of 40% of our outstanding Class E common stock) and a majority of the votes cast by our outstanding Class A common stock (subject to a quorum of a majority of outstanding shares of Class A common stock) is also obtained. Pursuant to the Stockholders Agreement, the Founder LLC will also vote or consent the share of Class F common stock in favor of amendments that are approved by 80% of the authorized members of our board of directors and 80% of the Trustees then in office (in each case, rounded up or down to the nearest whole number (any exact 0.5 rounding up)).
